<!-- page 1 -->
NOTICE OF PUBLIC HEARING AND AGENDA
NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS
Tuesday, June 11, 2019
ONE OR MORE SUPERVISORS MAY ATTEND VIA VIDEO AND/OR TELEPHONICALLY 
The Navajo County Board of Supervisors will hold a meeting open to the public on Tuesday, June 11, 2019, in the Supervisors’
Chamber in the Navajo County Governmental Complex, Holbrook, Arizona. The Board may vote to meet in a closed executive
session to discuss certain matters as indicated on the following agenda. The Board may meet in executive session pursuant to
A.R.S. § 38-431.03(A) (3) for legal advice on any item.
Persons with disabilities who need accommodation to attend or participate in the meeting may contact the Clerk of the Board’s office
at (928) 524-4053; at least 48 hours prior to the meeting so an accommodation can be arranged.
Items on the agenda may be considered out of order at the Chairman’s discretion. A copy of the agenda background material
provided to the Board, with the exception of items to be considered in executive session, is available for public inspection at Navajo
County website, www.navajocountyaz.gov 
9:00 a.m. Call To Order:  Invocation and Pledge of Allegiance
           
1. CALL TO THE PUBLIC: Individuals may address the Board on any relevant issue for up to
5 minutes. At the close of the call to the public, Board members may not respond to any
comments but may respond to criticism, ask staff to review a matter or ask that a matter be
placed on a future agenda.
 
 
 
2. CONSENT AGENDA: These items are considered to be routine or administrative in nature
and will be approved in a single motion. A Board member may request for any item to be
removed from the consent agenda and considered on the regular agenda:
 
 
a. Minutes: May 28, 2019  
 
b. Warrant & Voucher Report over $1000 May 2019  
 
c. Clerk of Superior Court Report May 2019  
 
d. Juvenile Probation Report May 2019  
 
e. Constable Reports: Show Low Precinct #5, May; and Pinetop-Lakeside Precinct #6, May 2019  
 
f. Justice Court Reports: Holbrook Precinct #1, Winslow Precinct #2, Snowflake Precinct #3,
Kayenta Precinct #4, Show Low Precinct #5, Pinetop-Lakeside Precinct #6 May 2019
 
 
g. Tax exemptions filed pursuant to ARS §42-11153 as a Request for Redemption of Waiver  
 
h. 2019 Revenue Sharing Application to the Tohono O'odham Nation by the Heber-Overgaard
Fire District for equipment in the amount of $45,614.17 and Letter of Support 
 
 
i. Distribution to the Schools from the Forest Fee allocation

<!-- page 2 -->
j. Memorandum of Understanding between the Sheriff's Office and the United States Marshal's
Service for joint law enforcement efforts
 
 
k. Amendment to Intergovernmental Agreement regarding the Employment of the Magistrate
Court Clerk
 
 
l. Amendment to the Intergovernmental Agreement for Court Collection and Criminal Justice
Services
 
 
m. Extension of Premises for American Legion Post #86 for July 6, 2019, for their 4th of July
Celebration at 2068 Lumber Valley Road in Overgaard
 
 
n. Contract signed by County Manager per Board of Supervisors Resolution: Quote from
SirsiDynix Technology Center for Navajo County Library District
 
 
o. Approval of 1 Back Tax Land Parcel sold over the counter in the amount of $615.35  
 
p. Approval of the use of the southwest corner of the Navajo County Complex for the annual
community July 4th Fireworks Display
 
 
q. Agreement between Navajo County and the Office of the Secretary of State for
Reimbursement for Systematic Alien Verification for Entitlements ("SAVE") Program Usage
 
 
3. COUNTY ATTORNEY:  
 
a. Presentation by Navajo County Attorney Brad Carlyon of a check to the Silver Creek Irrigation
District for amounts recovered on the District's behalf through civil asset forfeiture.
 
Presented By: Jason Moore/Brad Carlyon
 
4. ADMINISTRATIVE SERVICES:  
 
a. Consideration and adoption of the proposed Tentative Budget for Fiscal Year 2019-20 for
Navajo County. 
 
Presented By: Paige Peterson
 
b. Board of Directors Sessions for consideration and possible adoption of Tentative Fiscal Year
2019-20 budgets:  
1. Navajo County Public Health Services District
2. Navajo County Library District
3. Navajo County Flood Control District including LCR Flood Control Zone
4. White Mountain Lakes County Recreation Improvement District
5. Silver Creek County Road Improvement District
6. Victory Heights Road Maintenance District
7. Scott’s Pine Meadow County Road Improvement District
8. Shumway Road Improvement District
9. Bucking Horse Improvement District
10. Hilltop Drive Improvement District
11. Mountain View Improvement District
12. North Whistle Stop Loop Improvement District
13. Joseph City Street Lighting Improvement District
 
Presented By: Paige Peterson

<!-- page 3 -->
5. LIBRARY DISTRICT: Board of Directors Session:  
 
a. REGULAR AGENDA:  
 
1. Consideration of and acceptance of the Library Services and Technology Act grant, "Science
Excitement at  the Library" awarded to the Library District for $47,710.
 
Presented By: Geneva Durkee
 
2. Consideration of the Library District to administer a Library Services and Technology Act
Grant for the Hopi Public Library, Reading is Fun!, for $4,000.
 
Presented By: Geneva Durkee
 
6. PUBLIC WORKS:  
 
a. Consideration to award contract #F19-04-023 Annual Contract for Advertising and Printing to
Navajo County Publishers Inc., The Tribune.
 
Presented By: Jeanine Carruthers
 
b. PUBLIC HEARING: Consideration and adoption of Resolution Number 17-19 
approving/denying a Zone Change to change their RU-20 zoning on their property
to RU-5, APN 403-70-013B, a portion of Township 14 North, Range 23 East, Section 20, in
the Snowflake area.
 
Presented By: Sandra Phillips
 
c. PUBLIC HEARING: Consideration and adoption of Resolution Number 18-19 
approving/denying a Special Use Permit allowing for the construction of a fenced community
collection center adjacent to the current Pinetop-Lakeside Public Works building, that
will accommodate collection of household trash and provide recycling opportunities to the
public on a parcel which is zoned A-General, APN 212-38-020, a portion of Township 9
North, Range 22 East, Section 35, in the Pinetop-Lakeside area. 
 
Presented By: Sandra Phillips
 
d. NAVAJO COUNTY FLOOD CONTROL DISTRICT: Board of Directors Session:  
 
1. CONSENT AGENDA: The following item(s) are considered to be of a routine or
administrative in nature and will be approved as a group unless a member of the Board wishes
to discuss a particular item: 
 
 
a. Contract with Atkins in the amount of $49,923 to produce plans and specifications for the next
phase of channel improvements within the Sky Hi Subdivision to further mitigate flooding in
this area
 
 
7. COUNTY BUSINESS UPDATE: Report from County Manager, County Attorney and Board
members.
 
 
 
8. ADJOURN

<!-- page 4 -->
Dated: Friday, June 7, 2019 NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS 
Posted: 10:19 am By: MB                         
Melissa W. Buckley - Clerk of the Board

taxes that were due and payable before the petition was submitted may be refunded or abated.” The following

Board of Supervisors Regular 2.h.        
Meeting Date: 06/11/2019  
Title: Heber Overgaard Fire District Revenue Sharing Application to Tohono O'odham
Nation
Submitted By: Donna Hurstrom, Deputy Clerk of the Board
Department: Board of Supervisors
Motion before the Board:
2019 Revenue Sharing Application to the Tohono O'odham Nation by the Heber-Overgaard Fire
District for equipment in the amount of $45,614.17 and Letter of Support 
Background:
The Heber-Overgaard Fire District has filed a revenue sharing application to the Tohono O'odham
Nation for four (4) G1 4500 Self Contained Breathing Apparatus Mine Safety Appliances, ten
(10) 45-minute low profile air cylinders and four (4) masks for a total amount of $45,614.17.
Attachments
Grant Request 
Grant Reference Guide 
Quote for Equipment 
Letter of Support 
Form Review
Form Started By: Donna Hurstrom Started On: 05/29/2019 10:37 AM

<!-- page 457 -->
Heber-Overgaard Fire Department  
2061 Lumber Valley Road 
P.O. Box 1010 
Overgaard, AZ  85933 
(928) 535-4346 
 
 
 
About the District: 
The Heber-Overgaard Fire District (HOFD) is a Fire District providing fire, Emergency Medical Services and 
transport service to citizens in an unincorporated area of Navajo County. The District provides ambulance 
services to a 200 square mile area surrounding the communities of Heber-Overgaard, including 55 miles of 
State Highway and 460 miles of rural roads. Navajo County collects the property tax revenues for the Fire 
District and maintains the bank account for the District. The Fire District partners with Navajo County on many 
projects.  
HOFD has a significant wildland fire threat where 268 structures were lost during the Rodeo-Chediski fires and 
over 2,000 residents were evacuated for over two weeks. Last year the 377-fire consumed 5,414 acres and left 
the Heber-Overgaard area without power for three days. HOFD is seeking funding to reinforce the Firefighter 
respiratory protection program. 
 
Program Need, Population Beneficiaries: 
The priority funding area is Public Safety to purchase equipment for the protection of firefighters while in 
hazardous areas requiring respiratory protection. The Heber -Overgaard Fire District currently has a respiratory 
protection program in place. Numerous training hours are spent ensuring competency and situational awareness. 
This funding request bolsters a program that has been very successful protecting community first responders so 
they can protect life and property. Recently the community experienced a structure fire that also set the 
residence next door on fire. The available resources are few but the first responders worked diligently to save 
one of the buildings. The problem the first responders experienced was not enough breathing air to continue to 
work inside the deadly smoky environment. This request is to add four (4) self-contained breathing apparatus, 
four (4) masks and ten (10) cylinders that hold the safe breathing air for the firefighters.  
 
The population who will benefit is the community of the White Mountains for those who live, work and play. 
The equipment is solely intended to protect first responders in the event of fire, hazardous material release and 
any contaminated atmospheres where first responders need respiratory protection. 
 
Requests: 
The Heber-Overgaard Fire District has not received any 12% funding from the Tohono O’odham Nation. On 
October 19, 2017 The Heber-Overgaard Fire District received $60,000.00 from Gila River Community with a 
$25,000.00 in-kind contribution from the District for an ambulance.  
 
 
Contacts: 
William Dee McCluskey, Fire Chief    Jake Sample 
Chief@hofdaz.com      deputychief@hofdaz.com 
P.O. Box 1010       P.O. Box 1010 
Overgaard, AZ 85933      Overgaard, AZ 85933 
928-240-4149 cell      928-240-0145 cell 
928-535-3175 fax      928-535-3175 fax 
928-535-4346 office      928-535-4346 office

<!-- page 458 -->
Current source of funding: 
The Heber-Overgaard tax rate is 1.81% for a small community the Department prides itself on remaining 
fiscally responsible. This however does not allow for a major equipment purchase. The department will provide 
personnel and training to continue the program. Sustainability of the program will reside with the Heber-
Overgaard Fire District. HOFD would accept partial funding if necessary, for the cylinders that carry the 
breathing air.

Project Budget Detail: 
1. Quantity four (4) G1 4500 Self Contained Breathing Apparatus Mine Safety Appliances. G1 4500 pounds 
per square inch compressed air. Fire Service SCBA with quick connect cylinder, serviceable shoulder 
strap with chest strap. A metal tank band connected to adjustable lumbar swivel for wearer comfort. 
Voice amplification and an integrated thermal imaging device. The unit runs on a rechargeable lithium 
ion battery. 
2. Quantity ten (10) 45-minute low profile air cylinders.  
3. Quantity four (4) masks, one (1) large, one (1) small and two (2) medium. Masks include nose cup to 
reduce fogging of lens, a 4-point cloth harness that secures mask to head, and a fixed push to connect 
regulator connection. 
4. Taxes 
5. Delivery and in-service from manufacture representative are included with the purchase.

Re:  Revenue Sharing Application by the Heber-Overgaard Fire District 
 
To Whom It May Concern: 
 
Navajo County is in support of the Revenue Sharing Application submitted by the 
Heber-Overgaard Fire District for four (4) G1 4500 Self Contained Breathing Apparatus 
Mine Safety Appliances, ten (10) 45-minute low profile air cylinders and four (4) masks.  
This equipment will reinforce the Firefighter Respiratory Protection Program. 
 
The Heber-Overgaard Fire District currently has a Respiratory Protection Program in 
place, and numerous training hours are spent ensuring competency and situational 
awareness.  This funding request bolsters a program which has been very successful 
protecting community first responders so they can protect life and property. 
 
The requested funding will benefit the community of the White Mountains for those who 
live, work and play.  The equipment is solely intended to protect first responders in the 
event of fire, hazardous material release and any contaminated atmospheres where first 
responders need respiratory protection. 
 
Based on past experience with the Heber-Overgaard Fire District, Navajo County has 
confidence and is in full support of the revenue sharing application and encourages its 
acceptance.  
 
Sincerely, 
 
 
 
Dawnafe Whitesinger, Chairwoman 
Navajo County Board of Supervisors

Title: US Marshals Service MOU
Submitted For: David Clouse, Sheriff  Submitted By: Cheryl Powell, Deputy Chief

Motion before the Board:
Memorandum of Understanding between the Sheriff's Office and the United States Marshal's
Service for joint law enforcement efforts
Background:
The US Marshals Service has requested the assistance of the Sheriff's Office in conducting
compliance checks for sex offenders living within Navajo County.  This joint effort is a one time
operation.  Overtime will be reimbursed for our MCAT officers working the detail as well as a lap
top computer, scanner and camera.
This MOU will become effective upon the commencement of the detail and terminate upon the
conclusion of the operation.

Board of Supervisors Regular 2.k.        
Meeting Date: 06/11/2019  
Title: Amendment to IGA regarding the employment of the Magistrate Court Clerk
Submitted By: Donna Hurstrom, Deputy Clerk of the Board
Department: Board of Supervisors
Motion before the Board:
Amendment to Intergovernmental Agreement regarding the Employment of the Magistrate Court
Clerk
Background:
Navajo County has existing intergovernmental agreements with Winslow, Holbrook, Show Low,
and Pinetop-Lakeside to perform the clerical duties of the Magistrate Court.  The
intergovernmental agreement began on July 1, 2015, and is part of a larger initiative to benefit the
public by leveraging municipal and County resources together to provide justice services. The
proposed amendment updates the annual cost to perform these duties from $41,580 to $48,200 to
reflect the County’s actual cost increases including salary, state retirement, and healthcare.

Holbrook Amendment 
Pinetop Lakeside Amendment

Final Approval Date: 06/03/2019

<!-- page 472 -->
Page 1 of 2 
 
AMENDMENT TO 
INTERGOVERNMENTAL AGREEMENT FOR                                                                                                  
EMPLOYMENT OF THE MAGISTRATE COURT CLERK 
 
Between City of Winslow and Navajo County 
 
THIS AMENDMENT IS MADE THIS _______ DAY OF ______, 2019 between the City of Winslow, an Arizona 
Municipal Corporation, (“City”), and Navajo County, a political subdivision of the State of Arizona, 
(“County). 
WHEREAS, the City and the County previously entered into an Intergovernmental Agreement for 
Employment of the Magistrate Court Clerk on July 1, 2015 for the Justice Court Clerk and his/her clerical 
staff members to perform the clerical duties of the Magistrate Court. 
NOW, THEREFORE, in consideration of the mutual promises contained herein, the parties agree as follows: 
1. Section 3; ANNUAL CONTRACT FEE is amended as follows: 
The base rate of the ANNUAL CONTRACT FEE shall be adjusted to $48,200 to reflect the actual 
average cost of providing these services including salary and employment-related expenses. 
All other provisions of the IGA shall remain as agreed upon on July 1, 201 5, unless expressly revised or 
addressed hereinafter.  Any inconsistencies between this Amendment and the IGA shall be controlled by 
the provisions of this Amendment. 
IN WITNESS WHEREOF, the parties to this Amendment to Intergovernmental Agreement for Court 
Collection and Criminal Justice Services have caused their names to be affixed hereto by their duly 
authorized officers. 
 
 
 
 
NAVAJO COUNTY

<!-- page 480 -->
Board of Supervisors Regular 2.l.        
Meeting Date: 06/11/2019  
Title: Amendment to IGA for Court Collection and Criminal Justice Services
Submitted By: Donna Hurstrom, Deputy Clerk of the Board
Department: Board of Supervisors
Motion before the Board:
Amendment to the Intergovernmental Agreement for Court Collection and Criminal Justice
Services
Background:
Navajo County has existing intergovernmental agreements with Winslow, Holbrook,
Snowflake/Taylor, Show Low, and Pinetop-Lakeside for court collections and criminal justice
services for criminal misdemeanors.  The intergovernmental agreement began on July 1, 2011,
and is part of a larger initiative to benefit the public by leveraging municipal and County resources
together to provide justice services.  The proposed amendment includes the following changes: 
extends the agreement one (1) year beginning on July 1, 2019; includes an annual assessment of
$5,430 to account for expenses related to prosecution services for costs such as case management,
computers, software, and supplies; and amends the Withdrawal / Termination section of
agreement from 120 days written notice to 60 days’ written notice.   
Attachments
Winslow Amendment 
Holbrook Amendment 
Pinetop-Lakeside Amendment 
Show Low Amendment 
Snowflake Taylor Amendment

<!-- page 481 -->
Page 1 of 2 
 
AMENDMENT TO 
INTERGOVERNMENTAL AGREEMENT FOR COURT 
COLLECTION AND CRIMINAL JUSTICE SERVICES 
 
Between City of Winslow and Navajo County 
 
THIS AMENDMENT IS MADE THIS _______ DAY OF ______, 2019 between the City of Winslow, an Arizona 
Municipal Corporation, (“City”), and Navajo County, a political subdivision of the State of Arizona, 
(“County). 
WHEREAS, the City and the County previously entered into an Intergovernmental Agreement for Court 
Collection and Criminal Justice Services on July 1, 2011 for court collections and criminal justice services 
for criminal misdemeanors; and 
WHEREAS, the City and County desire to extend the term of the Intergovernmental Agreement for Court 
Collection and Criminal Justice Services for an additional one (1) year term. 
NOW, THEREFORE, in consideration of the mutual promises contained herein, the parties agree as follows: 
1. Section 2; TERM / EFFECTIVE DATE is amended as follows: 
The term of this IGA shall be for one (1) year effective July 1, 2019. 
2. Section 4; MUNICIPALITY’S FEE is amended as follows: 
The Municipality’s Fee shall include an annual assessment of $5,430 to account for expenses 
related to prosecution services including, but not limited to:  case management software, IT 
equipment, software, supplies, printing, and postage.  
3. Section 5; WITHDRAWAL / TERMINATION is amended as follows: 
The Navajo County Attorney’s Office has the right to withdraw from the Agreement's provision 
that it provide prosecution services for any criminal case arising within the municipal limits upon 
giving the municipality sixty (6 0) days prior notice. Upon termination of the Navajo County 
Attorney's Office obligation to prosecute under this Agreement, the municipality will be 
responsible for prosecuting all criminal cases that occur within their municipal boundaries. U pon 
termination of the Navajo County Attorney's Office obligation to prosecute criminal cases 
occurring within the jurisdiction of the municipal court, Navajo County shall pay to the 
municipality a pro-rated refund of the municipality's payment to Navajo County for prosecution 
services. In the event of termination of prosecution services, the Navajo County Attorney's Office 
shall cooperate with the municipal prosecutor to ensure a smooth transition of cases. 
 
The Navajo Coun ty Public Defender or Legal Defender has the right to withdraw from the 
Agreement's provision that it provide prosecution services for any criminal case arising within the 
municipal limits upon giving the municipality sixty (60) days prior notice. Upon termination of the 
Public Defender or Legal Defender's obligation to provide defense services under this Agreement, 
the municipality will be responsible for providing defense services in all criminal cases that occur 
within their municipal boundaries. Upon termination of the Navajo County Public Defender or 
Legal Defender’s  obligation to prosecute criminal cases occurring within the jurisdiction of the 
municipal court, Navajo County shall pay to the municipality a pro -rated refund of the 
municipality's payment to Navajo County for defense  services. In the event of termination of

<!-- page 482 -->
Page 2 of 2 
 
defense  services, the Navajo County Public Defender or Legal Defender shall cooperate with the 
municipal defense to ensure a smooth transition of cases.  
All other provisions of the IGA shall remain as agreed upon on July 1, 2011, unless expressly revised or 
addressed hereinafter.  Any inconsistencies between this Amendment and the IGA shall be controlled by 
the provisions of this Amendment. 
IN WITNESS WHEREO F, the parties to this Amendment to Intergovernmental Agreement for Court 
Collection and Criminal Justice Services have caused their names to be affixed hereto by their duly 
authorized officers. 
 
NAVAJO COUNTY 
  
By:________________________________  
Chairman Board of Supervisors  
ATTEST:  
 
_________________________________  
Clerk of Board  
APPROVED AS TO FORM AND CONTENT: 
  
_________________________________  
Attorney

Board of Supervisors Regular 2.q.        
Meeting Date: 06/11/2019  
Title: Agreement with Secretary of State for reimbursement for SAVE
Submitted By: Doris Clark, Recorder
Department: Recorder
Motion before the Board:
Agreement between Navajo County and the Office of the Secretary of State for Reimbursement
for Systematic Alien Verification for Entitlements ("SAVE") Program Usage
Background:
This agreement would allow the County Recorders to have access to the SAVE Program for the
purpose of verifying the citizenship and immigration status information of non-citizen and
naturalized or derived U.S. citizen registrants when they register to vote.

<!-- page 520 -->
Board of Supervisors Regular 3.a.        
Meeting Date: 06/11/2019  
Title: Check Presentation to the Silver Creek Irrigation District
Submitted By: Jason Moore, Deputy County Attorney
Department: County Attorney
Presented By: Jason Moore/Brad Carlyon
Subject:
Presentation by Navajo County Attorney Brad Carlyon of a check to the Silver Creek Irrigation
District for amounts recovered on the District's behalf through civil asset forfeiture.
Background: 
The Navajo County Attorney's Office has recovered at least $215,000 for the Silver Creek
Irrigation District through use of the Arizona's civil asset forfeiture laws in order help make the
land owners and taxpayers within the SCID boundaries whole for the losses they suffered as a
result of thefts that occurred between 2011 and 2016.

Board of Supervisors Regular 4.a.        
Meeting Date: 06/11/2019  
Title: Tentative Budget 2019-20
Submitted By: Melissa Buckley, Clerk of the Board of Supervisors
Department: Administrative Services Division: Finance
Presented By: Paige Peterson
Motion before the Board:
Consideration and adoption of the proposed Tentative Budget for Fiscal Year 2019-20 for Navajo
County. 
Background:
As required by statute, a full and complete statement of the county's financial affairs for the
preceding fiscal year are included. Additionally, an estimate of the amounts required to meet the
county's expenditures of fiscal year 2019-20 is included and has been presented on a summary
schedule of estimated revenues and expenditures in accordance with the Arizona Office of the
Auditor General's prescribed format. 
Recommendation: 
Staff recommends adoption of the tentative budget for Fiscal Year 2019-20.

Board of Supervisors Regular 5.a.1.        
Meeting Date: 06/11/2019  
Title: Acceptance of the LSTA grant award "Science Excitement in the Library"
Submitted For: Geneva Durkee, Library District Director 
Submitted By: Geneva Durkee, Library District Director
Department: NC Library District
Presented By: Geneva Durkee
Motion before the Board:
Consideration of and acceptance of the Library Services and Technology Act grant, "Science
Excitement at  the Library" awarded to the Library District for $47,710.
Background:
With this award, the Library District will seek to bring exciting science programs to the library
targeting teens, adults, and seniors. The Library District will hold STEM programs in the member
libraries, produce science kits for checkout, increase holdings of new nonfiction scientific
materials, and enhance the science fiction and fantasy collections with new books.   Some
programs the patrons will use while visiting the library, others will be facilitated by either
community library staff or library district staff. Still others will be available for checkout to
patrons for interactions with their children.  These are presented, in part, in anticipation of the
large number of homeschooled students within the county. 
Concerning the programs we will hold in thirteen member libraries, we intend to utilize materials
or ideas from StarNet and Nasa@YourLibrary to showcase items that people don’t normally have
available in their homes. One example is the Orion SpaceProbe II 76mm Equitorial Reflector
Telescope Kit. Another is the Google Expeditions Kit to feature augmented/virtual reality
programs. 
Our circulating kits will include for example, an IQCrew Science Discovery Set with a 20x
Microscope and an AMSCOPE-Kids 15-150x 300x70mm Compact Telescope among
others, for the thirteen libraries. 
 
 One program that will add a fun element to the summer reading program is a Passport to the
Planets where people travel to a participating library to get information on one of the planets and a
stamp on their passport, visiting eight libraries to get all their stamps and learn about the eight
planets.
The libraries' science fiction and fantasy books need to be increased and upgraded.
These are popular, appealing genres that can stimulate interest in scientific topics.
 
Recommendation:

<!-- page 547 -->
To accept the Library Services and Technology Act grant, "Science Excitement at  the Library"
awarded to the Library District for $47,710.
Fiscal Impact
Fiscal Year: 2019-20
Budgeted Y/N: Y

Fiscal Impact:
This is a grant that only requires an in-kind match.

Request Information

This is the application for LSTA general grants. Follow the links below for LSTA mini-grants:

STEAM In Your Library

General Project Information 
  Choose One Category   
 
  
Informal Education 
    
  Project Title   
 
  
Science Excitement at the Library     
  
Briefly summarize the project, by completing the following: The goal of this project 
is______. Funds will be used to______. Community members will benefit because 
__________.  
  
 
  
The goal of this project is to bring exciting science programs to the library and to make 
kits available for checkout to increase people’s knowledge and awareness of STEM. Funds 
will be used to purchase the kits and program materials, as well as collections of new 
nonfiction science materials and science fiction and fantasy books. Community members 
will benefit because the audiences in our rural communities ranging from elementary age 
to senior adults will have access to an array of technologies through our libraries not 
easily available anywhere else, and their new-found knowledge will help them to inspire 
young children to become excited about science and technology.   
  
  Select a Primary Project Intent. Please Check one.   
 
  
Improve users' general knowledge and skills (Lifelong Learning) 
    
  Amount of grant funds you are requesting:   
 
  
47710     
  For your library, is this project New or Continuing?   
 
  
New   
Needs Assessment 
  What audiences are you targeting with this project and why?   
 
  
This project will target teens, adults, seniors, parents and grandparents from all socio -
economic levels. The grant focuses on this audience because everyone can benefit from 
greater awareness and knowledge of science. Adults can inspire the younger generations 
with their enthusiasm and learning, thereby supporting educators’ efforts also. Additional 
exposure to science in the library may inspire teens to pursue science-related careers.   
  
  What community need or opportunity does your project address?   
 
  
For many people, learning about science stops when they finish their formal education, 
often for lack of opportunity to be connected to a STEM environment. Navajo County does 
not have museums focusing on science and technology, and few to none other venues to 
pursue an interest in science. Fascinating developments and the tools of science and 
STEM may bypass adults and seniors.

<!-- page 551 -->
How do you know this is a need?   
 
  
Read On’s MapLit shows education levels are quite low in Navajo County. 50% of 
Winslow’s population has a high school/GED education or less; Holbrook is 44%; Show 
Low shows 42% in MapLit. 62% of Whiteriver’s population has a high school/GED 
education or less. Towncharts.com shows that Navajo County is second to last in AZ for 
the number of people with at least a high school education and is third to last for people 
with a B.A. degree or higher. Navajo County ranks dead last in the percentage of B.A. 
degrees in Science and Engineering. Such a large portion of the population is not getting 
any formal exposure to STEM after the elementary grades or high school, and therefore is 
missing out, not only for themselves in scientific knowledge, but also not being apt to 
inspire and support the preschoolers and elementary grades to learn about science and 
technology. 
 
AIMS science test scores show of 36 schools only 15 had passing percentages 50% or 
higher. Only three high schools in the county had a 50% or higher score. The Library 
District will include teens, i.e. middle and high school students, in our focus as well as 
adults and seniors. 
  
  
  How does this project relate to your library’s mission or other plans (such as a technology 
plan)?    
 
  
Navajo County Library District exists to aid and improve the services, materials, and 
technology provided by the member public libraries in the County to make people’s lives 
better. This project’s emphasis on exposing people to the enrichment and excitement of 
science will increase their knowledge during meaningful and relevant library visits to our 
participating libraries and their use of our collections and science materials.   
  
Project Outcomes 
  What specific change(s) do you intend to achieve with this project?    
 
  
We plan to leverage our public libraries to provide close and exciting encounters with 
advanced scientific tools, augmented and virtual reality, and equipment for our 
population, and secondly, to create many more enthusiastic readers of science fiction and 
fantasy which can stimulate an interest in scientific topics.   
  
  Briefly explain why you have selected this outcome, tying it to the Needs Assessment 
provided.   
 
  
The implication of our needs assessment is that lack of knowledge and exposure to STEM 
materials and books is widespread in our county, and that older population groups are not 
equipped to support the learning of preschoolers and elementary students. Many of our 
patrons do not have the ability to travel outside of their small communities. These 
programs would allow the world to come to them enriching their lives.   
  
Project Activities 
  What will you do, and how will you do it? Give specific information about the types of 
activities, number of programs, or resources you'll make available.   
 
  
The Library District will hold STEM programs in the member libraries, produce science kits 
for checkout, increase holdings of new nonfiction scientific materials, and enhance the 
science fiction and fantasy collections with new books. The programs are diverse in their 
applications. Some will be passive programs the patrons use while visiting the lib rary, 
others will be facilitated by either community library staff or library district staff. Still 
others will be available for checkout to patrons for interactions with their children. These 
are presented, in part, in anticipation of the large number of homeschooled students

<!-- page 552 -->
within the county.  
Concerning the programs we will hold in thirteen member libraries, we intend to utilize 
materials or ideas from StarNet and Nasa@YourLibrary to showcase items that people 
don’t normally have available in their homes. One example is the Orion SpaceProbe II 
76mm Equitorial Reflector Telescope Kit. Another is the Google Expeditions Kit to feature 
augmented/virtual reality programs. One program that will add a fun element to SRP is a 
Passport to the Planets where people travel to a participating library to get information on 
one of the planets and a stamp on their passport, visiting eight libraries to get all their 
stamps and learn about the eight planets. 
 
Our circulating kits will include for example, an IQCrew Science Discovery Set with a 20x 
Microscope and a AMSCOPE-Kids 15-150x 300x70mm Compact Telescope among others, 
for the thirteen libraries.  
 
Another aspect will significantly update our collections of materials on STEM topics. For 
fiction readers we will greatly increase or upgrade our science fiction and fantasy 
collections in our libraries. These genres have been neglected in most of our libraries due 
to lack of funds, and yet these genres are appealing to many teen and adult readers and 
can stimulate interest in many scientific topics. 
  
  What steps has your library taken to prepare for this project?   
 
  
The Library District library technician and some of our member library staff have attended 
State Library training workshops -the YA Summit and NASA@YourLibrary, among others - 
to prepare and train others to facilitate these programs. We have researched the StarNet 
website and others regarding effective science programs. The Library District has 
discussed with key member library staff their input on what elements they would like to 
have for their libraries. Research on desirable equipment and checkout kits for pricing and 
components took several days.   
  
  Why did you choose this approach for your project? Did you consider any alternative 
approaches?   
 
  
This project will use several approaches, namely public in-library programming actively 
led by staff, but also because Navajo County has lots of one-person or volunteer-run 
libraries, the Library District will provide a significant number of scientific kits and 
materials that can be checked out, a more passive approach. The patrons will still have 
the benefit of these items without the necessity of the staff being a part of every 
program. Our consideration of alternatives made us develop this multi-faceted proposal.   
  
  List at least one product or activity that will help you meet the outcome. All products and 
activities that are a part of your project should be linked to an outcome.   
 
  
1. Programs from NASA@yourlibrary provide access to equipment to rural and tribal 
communities that allow them to learn the mysteries of our solar system and universe, 
teaching all ages how NASA observes space. 
2. The Augmented/Virtual Reality equipment allows students to experience technology 
that is available to children in urban areas. Many seniors can’t travel great distances. The 
use of AR/VR opens expanded travel to them. 
3. Kits for in-library exploration and those for checkout open the world of science, 
promoting knowledge that provides children with a background desired by local 
employers. 
  
  
Project Evaluation

<!-- page 553 -->
Describe what tool(s) or method(s) you will use to evaluate each product or activity listed. 
Examples include pre- and post-assessments, surveys, focus groups, interviews, and 
observations. 
  
 
  
Navajo County Library District will utilize the survey recommended by Jaime Ball (and 
IMLS) to give to the participants of the programs and gather anecdotal observations. We 
will also track usage of the new collections and circulating kits.

Timeline/Schedule of Activities 
  
Provide a timeline that includes planning, implementation and evaluation, and indicates 
when funds will be spent. Remember, funds are not available before May 17, 2019, and 
must be spent by September 4, 2020. 
  
 
  
Attend LSTA Grant Recipient Workshop in May 2019;  
Library District staff holds planning meetings with member librarians in May or June 2019.  
Materials for the Science and Space programming are purchased in May-September 2019. 
New science fiction and fantasy books are purchased from June 2019 to July 2020. 
Process and catalog new materials from June 2019 to July 2020. 
Participating libraries hold programs in 2019 & 2020 during grant period. 
Assemble circulating kits from July to January 2020. 
Produce publicity on programs and new materials through social media ongoing through 
grant period. 
Gather statistics periodically throughout the grant period. 
Evaluate the effectiveness of and modify the various library programs as needed. 
Ensure all funds are expended by August 1, 2020. 
File the final report on the grant by September 2020. 
  
  
Sustainability 
  If successful, will you continue this project once the grant period has ended? If so, how 
will you sustain it?   
 
  
Much of the grant elements will be sustained because the equipment purchased 
(microscopes, telescopes, binoculars, compasses, VR equipment) is durable and should 
last for a long time. Librarians will have the kits for in-library programs to use in 
subsequent years. The new books will also remain available for several years and we can 
continue to add to those collections.   
  
Budget Information  
Project Budget

Navajo County Library District requests funds to purchase the science kits for use in 
library programs and to purchase library materials/kits to circulate in the fourteen 
libraries. Some of these kits include books recommended by NASA to accompany their 
programs. The kits will provide binoculars, compasses, telescopes, and microscopes, 
among other items. One is a coding workshop set. The most expensive item but perhaps 
the most intriguing is the Google Expeditions kit – a 10 person kit for $4800.

Board of Supervisors Regular 5.a.2.        
Meeting Date: 06/11/2019  
Title: Approval to administer LSTA grant "Reading is Fun" for Hopi Library
Submitted For: Geneva Durkee, Library District Director 
Submitted By: Geneva Durkee, Library District Director
Department: NC Library District
Presented By: Geneva Durkee
Motion before the Board:
Consideration of the Library District to administer a Library Services and Technology Act Grant
for the Hopi Public Library, Reading is Fun!, for $4,000.
Background:
The Library District applied for this LSTA grant "Reading is Fun" for the Hopi Public Library to
acquire boardbooks, picture books, pop-up books, easy readers, elementary high
interest/low vocabulary books, dialogic reading tools and beginning chapter books for the
new branch library recently opened. The goal is to improve reading readiness of infants,
toddlers, preschoolers,a nd elementary age children. We will also acquire simple Hopi
language books, albeit these are few in number that are published. 
Programming at the new Polacca Branch Library will be designed to encourage reading by
hosting interactive reading programs, family reading challenges, reading partner
challenges (reading partners to include participants from different generations, i.e., a
parent and child, grandparent and grandchild, aunt or uncle and a child/youth, etc.). The
idea of having intergenerational reading is to encourage participants to read together,
whether it be reading out loud or to themselves and retelling the story they just read.
The award is to the Hopi Public Library but the funds will be administered by the Library
District.
 
Recommendation: 
Approval for the Library District to administer a Library Services and Technology Act Grant for
the Hopi Public Library, Reading is Fun!, for $4,000

Project Information  
  Collections and Programming (CAP) Grants are $4,000 grants to expand collections for a 
target audience and to increase access to those collections through programs. Program ideas

<!-- page 566 -->
include, but are not limited to, lectures, cultural events, workshops, facilitated discussions, 
field trips, and library "outposts". CAP grants afford opportunities for library staff to reach out 
to community partners. The primary purpose of the grant is to provide resources for 
community members with defined needs such as at-risk children, the aging population, people 
having difficulty using the library, and others targeted in the LSTA priorities.  
Applications are due by March 8, 2019. 
This grant is paid for with LSTA funds and all federal regulations apply. For eligibility 
requirements, refer to the current grant guidelines. 
      
Project Description 
  What audience(s) are you targeting with this CAP grant and why?   
 
  
This CAP grant will target infants, toddlers, preschoolers and elementary age children. In 
essence the Hopi Library must also include the parents, relatives and caregivers of these 
children since they will be our partners in improving reading readiness and overcoming 
the challenges of the reluctant and struggling readers. We have chosen this audience 
because we have opened a new branch library that is woefully lacking in materials for 
these age groups, and certainly does not have enough new materials to cover the 
spectrum of reading levels and interests that will entice the pre-readers, beginning 
readers, and struggling readers to enjoy their encounters with books.   
  
  What specific change do you intend to achieve with this CAP grant?   
 
  
The primary change Hopi Library staff plans to achieve is to increase the time that babies, 
toddlers, and beginning readers spend being read to or reading. If we can turn some 
reluctant readers into avid readers and struggling readers into fluent readers those wou ld 
be major achievements as well.   
  
  What collection materials does your library plan to provide to achieve your intended 
outcome?   
 
  
The collection materials to be provided are boardbooks, picture books, pop-up books, 
easy readers, elementary high interest/low vocabulary books, dialogic reading tools and 
beginning chapter books. We will acquire simple Hopi language books, albeit these are 
few in number that are published.   
  
  Describe the programming your library plans to provide to support the collections.   
 
  
The programming will be designed to encourage reading by hosting interactive reading 
programs, family reading challenges, reading partner challenges (reading partners to 
include participants from different generations, i.e., a parent and child, grandparent  and 
grandchild, aunt or uncle and a child/youth, etc.). The idea of having intergenerational 
reading is to encourage participants to read together, whether it be reading out loud or to 
themselves and retelling the story they just read. Intergenerational reading will also 
stretch the children’s exposure to different types of subjects and reading levels, and of 
course adults modeling reading behavior is one of the great ways to achieve our desired 
change. Additional programming may be special programs featuring local authors, 
musicians and storytellers.   
  
Project Personnel 
  Identify the project director, and explain why he or she is appropriate for this project    
 
  
Geneva Durkee, Navajo County Library District Director, because she works closely with 
the Hopi Public Library staff and is aware of our needs. Even more she is aware of the 
difficulties concerning the Hopi Tribe's purchasing. We do not have accounts with book 
jobbers to get good discounts whereas she can more efficiently and economical ly spend

<!-- page 567 -->
the grant. The Hopi Library program is also very short-staffed, and as the county librarian 
can propel the project forward.   
  Provide a brief resume for any consultant for whom you are requesting LSTA funds.   
 
  
 
  
  If you are partnering with other organizations for this project, describe each partner’s 
role. Enter N/A if not applicable.   
 
  
Navajo County Library District, Holbrook, AZ. The director will administer the grant, 
purchase the materials, and the Library District staff will process and catalog them from 
that office, so they are in the integrated catalog.  
 
First Mesa Youth Center, Polacca, AZ. This facility houses the Hopi Branch Library. Their 
partnership will enable us to use other parts of the facility to host our reading programs 
and their staff will lend support to our activities, and include them in their publicity.  
  
  
Timeline/Schedule of Activities 
  Provide a brief timeline for planning and implementing your project. A final report is due 
September 4, 2020.   
 
  
Attend LSTA Grant Recipient Workshop in May 2019;  
Purchase new boardbooks, toddler books, easy readers, hi-lo books and others from June 
2019 to May, 2020 
Process and catalog new materials from July 2019 to May, 2020 
Schedule and hold programs in the library for inter-generational reading, family reading 
challenges, and reading partner challenges throughout the grant period. 
Publicize the availability of these new children's books through social media from July 
2019 and ongoing throughout the grant period. 
Gather statistics through the automation system. 
File the final report on grant by September 2020. 
  
  
Budget Information  
Project Budget 
  Please explain how you will use the $4,000 allotted for this CAP grant.   
 
  
The majority of the funds $3400) will be spent for the books for children. $240 in travel 
funds will allow Dinah Pongyesva, Hopi Library Technician to attend the grant workshop in 
Phoenix in May. The indirect costs at 10% ($360) are required by Navajo County.

The applicant is requesting a Zone Change from R-20 (Rural-20 acre minimum to R-5 (Rural - 5

parcels and one 10 acre parcel.   If approved by the Board of Supervisors, the applicant will then
apply for a Minor Land Division.  The Navajo County Comprehensive Plan, characterizes the area
as Rural Edge. The character of Rural edge advises for lower density residential development,
adjacent to Community Village and within 2 miles of a highway, projected future routes or other
significant road.    The proposed project fits within the recommendations of the Navajo County
Comprehensive Plan.  Planning & Zoning Commission recommended approval to the Board by a
vote of 6 yeas and 0 nay.

building, septic system, grading, and drainage permits.

5/16/19 
 
ATTENDANCE 
P & Z Commissioners

1. Brian Shields

Meeting held at the Navajo County Board of Supervisors Chambers, Holbrook, Arizona – 
Time:  6:02 to  
 
Chairman Chuck Teetsel called the meeting of the Navajo County Planning & Zoning 
Commission to order and explained the meeting procedures to the public.  Mr. Teetsel 
then led the pledge of Allegiance. 
 
ITEM #1 – CASE #19-24 ZONE CHANGE, DISTRICT V:   Discussion and possible 
action on a request by TST Holdings LLC for a Zone Change from existing Special 
Development (SD) known as Starlight Ridge Estates Unit III to A -General on 20.26 & 
32.10 acres known as APN 212 -05-007K and 212 -05-007L, respectively, a portion of 
Township 9 North, Range 22 East, Section 8 in the Show Low area. 
 
Item tabled at applicant’s request. 
 
ITEM #2 – CASE #19-25 ZONE CHANGE, DISTRICT III:  Discussion and possible 
action on a request by Elva & Mary Dreos for a Zone Change, from existing RU -20 
zoning on a 20.15-acre parcel to RU -5 zoning on APN 403 -70-013B, a portion of 
Township 14 North, Range 23 East, Section 20 of the Gila and Salt River Meridian in the 
Concho area.   
 
Sandra introduced the item, detailing the area was off of Hay Hollow Road. The 
applicant is requesting the Zone Change so that family can also be close by.  Staff had 
discussed the project with the applicant, re commending that she reach out to her 
neighbors. The applicant is also present to answer questions. Staff received no 
objections, and the applicant has letters of support from neighbors to present to the

<!-- page 585 -->
board. Staff does not o bject to the five-acre zoning, especially given how close it is to 
Hay Hollow Road. 
 
Elva Dreos was able to speak . She said she had two letters from neighbors and listed 
their names and showed the board where they were in relation to her property. 
 
Steve Strand also came up to speak and wished to create more funding for the county 
and their own business.  
 
Chairman asked Mr. Strand how far away he lived from the property, Mr. Strand replied 
about two miles as the crow flies. 
 
Commissioner Rhoades asked staff about the lot splits, Sandra clarified that the zone 
change was for RU-5, and any splits larger than 5 acres would be allowed. 
 
Chairman asked if anyone wanted to speak for or against the applicant, and then asked 
if the Commission had any questions for staff. 
 
Sandra clarified once more that staff had no objections to 5-acre minimum zoning due to 
the location along Hay Hollow Road. 
 
Commissioner Rhoades  made a motion to recommend Approval.  < Com. Murph > 
seconded the motion.  Motion 6-0 passed. 
 
 
ITEM # 3 – CASE #19-26 SPECIAL USE PERMIT, DISTRICT IV : Discussion and 
possible action on a request from Stephanie Crain to operate a residential group home 
within a 0.98 acre lot; APN 409-10-223, a portion of Township 10 North, Range 20 East, 
Section 13 of the Gila and Salt River Meridian in the Timberland Acres area.  
 
Item Removed by Staff. 
 
ITEM #4 – CASE #19-27 SPECIAL USE PERMIT, DISTRICT II:  Discussion and 
possible action on a request from Justin Miller to expand the existing Mobile Home park 
on 5.0 acres o n APN 103 -30-017C, a portion of Township 19 North, Range 16 East, 
Section 20 of the Gila and Salt River Meridian in the Bushman Acres area.  
 
Item Postponed by Applicant. 
  
ITEM #5 – CASE #19-28 SPECIAL USE PERMIT, DISTRICT V:  Discussion and 
possible action on a request from the Town of Pinetop Lakeside to construct and operate 
a recycling center on a 1-acre parcel that is zoned A-General APN 212-38-020, a portion 
of Township 9 North, Range 22 East, Section 35 of the Gila and Salt River Meridian in 
the Pinetop area. 
 
Sandra introduced the project, stating that the property was one acre and owned by 
Town of Pinetop/Lakeside, showing where the Town and County boundaries. She also 
pointed out where more Town buildings were in the area , such as the Public Works 
Building.

<!-- page 586 -->
Sandra introduced the applicant’s plans, showed where the postings were  made, and 
stated that staff had not received any objections from the public.  
 
The Public Works Director for Town of Pinetop/Lakeside explained the need for this 
project. He showed the Commission the issues they were dealing with, stat ing that the 
place would be staffed so th at illegal dumping issues could be dealt with. ADEQ noted 
that the site would be able to house household hazardous wastes like batteries and so 
forth.  He further went to note measures that will be used to make the facility windproof. 
ADEQ requires that the fence be animal -proof. Plans to improve the road were also 
discussed. 
 
Chuck Teetsel asked if the facility would be like a transfer station 
 
PWD stated that it would be similar, to stop dumping in Town parks.  
 
Randy asked if they were incorporating a compactor. 
PWD replied no and stated if the facility needed to get larger they would be moving.  
 
Randy asked about the fence. 
PWD clarified plans for the fence.

STAFF REPORT 
BOARD OF SUPERVISORS 
 
Hearing Date: June 11, 2019 
Case No:  19-25 
Action:   Zone Change 
Applicant/Owner: Elva & Mary Dreos 
Project Name: Dreos Zone Change 
PARCEL INFORMATION  
 Address: 5131 Concho Ocho Road 
 
 APN:  403-70-013B 
 
 Legal Desc: Township 14 Nort h, Range 23 East, Section 20 of the Gila and Salt River  
  Meridian in the Snowflake Area. 
 
 District: III 
 
 Area:  Snowflake, AZ 
 
 Parcel Size: Approximately 20.15 Acres 
 
STATED REASON FOR REQUEST:  
 
Elva and Mary Dreos are requesting a Zone Change to change their RU-20 zoning on their property to 
RU-5 zoning. 
 
GENERAL CHARACTER OF NEIGHBORHOOD:  
 
The subject property is zoned RU-20.  The property is surrounded in all directions by RU-20 zoned 
properties with single-family residences to the north and south of the subject property.

<!-- page 596 -->
The applicant is requesting a zoning change from R-20 (Rural- 20 acre minimum) to R-5 (Rural – 5 acre 
minimum) for the purpose of dividing the existing 20 acre parcel into four (4) five (5) acre parcels.  Should 
the Commission approve the zone change, the applicant will then apply for a Minor Land Division.   
 
The subject parcel is located northeast of Snowflake in a decidedly rural area with predominately large (20-
40 acre) parcels and scattered residential uses.   
 
 
 
 ZONING DISTRICTS: 
 
ARTICLE 4 – (RU-20) – RURAL ZONING DISTRICT 
    TWENTY (20) ACRES PER DWELLING UNIT 
 
Section 401 – Purpose  
The primary purpose of this district is to conserve and protect open land uses, foster orderly growth in 
rural areas, and prevent urban agricultural land use conflicts.  The purpose of requiring large 
minimum parcels of not less than twenty (20) acres in area is discourage small lot or residential 
subdivisions where public facilities such as water, sewage disposal, parks and playgrounds, and 
governmental services such as police and fire protection are not available or could not reasonably be 
made currently available.  Uses permitted in this zoning district include both farm and non-farm 
residential uses, farms, and recreational and institutional uses.

<!-- page 597 -->
ARTICLE 6 – (RU-5) – RURAL ZONING DISTRICT 
    FIVE (5) ACRES PER DWELLING UNIT 
 
Section 601 – Purpose  
The primary purpose of this district is to conserve and protect open land uses, foster orderly growth in 
rural areas, and prevent urban agricultural land use conflicts. The purpose of requiring large minimum 
parcels of not less than five (5) acres in area is to discourage small lot or residential subdivisions 
where public facilities such as water, sewage disposal, parks and playgrounds, and governmental 
services such as police and fire protection are not available or could not be made currently available. 
Uses permitted in this zoning district include both farm and non-farm residential uses, farms, and 
recreational and institutional uses. 
 
COMPREHENSIVE PLAN:  
4.0 Land Use Element 
 
4.4 Character Areas and Overlay 
 
4.4.3 Rural Edge 
 
The purpose of the Rural Edge character area is to provide lower density residential development 
adjacent to Community Village. The character area is typically designated for property adjacent to 
existing or planned areas of higher density and for property generally within two miles of a highway, 
Projected Future Routes, or other significant roads in the area.  
 
A development may occur in any zoning district within the character area, provided it complies with 
the subdivision regulations. All zoning districts may be applied for within this character area via a 
zone change request. 
 
ANALYSIS: 
  
As of May 30th, 2019, no concerns have been raised by notified neighbors within the area.  The 
applicant has sent letters to nearby properties and has received responses of support from three (3) of 
the neighbors.   
 
During the May 16
th, 2019 Planning and Zoning Commission Meeting, Planning and Zoning Staff 
recommended that the Navajo County Planning and Zoning Commission make the recommendation 
of approval of the Zone Change to the Navajo County Board of Supervisors, subject to stipulations.  
In that meeting, the Commission did unanimously make the recommendation to the Board of 
Supervisors, subject to the stipulations laid out within this Staff Report. 
 
FINDINGS OF FACT: 
 
1. The item has been properly noticed and posted in compliance with Arizona Revised Statutes and 
Article 29. 
The legal notice for this item has been printed in the local ne wspaper, in compliance with Arizona 
Revised Statutes and Article 29 of the Zoning Ordinance.  Notic es were sent to all parcels within a

<!-- page 598 -->
300’ boundary from the parcel and public notices were posted on the property. 
2. The change is in compliance with the Comprehensive Plan. 
The character area in which this  parcel is located allows for a ll zoning districts to be applied for via 
zone change requests. 
3. The change will have minimal adverse effects on the public heal th, safety, and general welfare 
of properties in the vicinity of the proposed use. 
Due to the subject property’s lo cation along Hay Hollow Road, t he major thoroughfare for the area, 
there will likely be little to no adverse effects seen as a result of Zone Change. 
 
PUBLIC WORKS AND OTHER COUNTY DEPARTMENT’S COMMENTS: 
COUNTY ATTORNEY:  
No objection, as long as the applicant complies with all rules and regulations of Navajo County and the State of 
Arizona. 
          I n itial: B.S.C.  Date:   
      
ENGINEERING AND TRAFFIC: 
Engineering staff has reviewed the proposed Zone Change and has no Engineering objections. 
Initial: W.R.B. Date:  
   
DRAINAGE & FLOOD CONTROL: 
The Flood Control staff has reviewed the proposed Zone Change and has no objections. 
Initial: J.R. Date:  
   
PLANNING AND ZONING: 
The Planning and Zoning Staff has reviewed the proposed Zone Change and has no objections. 
Initial: S.P. Date:  
 
   
PLANNING AND ZONING RECOMMENDATION:  
 
The Planning and Zoning Commission has unanimously recommended approval of the Zone Change to 
the Navajo County Board of Supervisors, subject to the following conditions: 
 
1. Access easements to all parcels to be dedicated on Survey prepa red by Arizona Professional Land 
Surveyor. 
2. Maintain setbacks of 20 ft rear, 30 feet side yard, and 20 ft f ront yard setback and maximum lot 
coverage of ten percent (10%). 
3. The applicant shall obtain all required Navajo County permits, including but not limited to, building, 
septic system, grading, and drainage permits.

Board of Supervisors Regular 6.c.        
Meeting Date: 06/11/2019  
Title: PTLS Collection Center Special Use Permit - District V 
Submitted For: John Osgood, Public Works Director 
Submitted By: Sandra Phillips, Assistant County Engineer/Flood Control Manager
Department: Public Works Division: Planning & Zoning
Presented By: Sandra Phillips
Motion before the Board:
PUBLIC HEARING: Consideration and adoption of Resolution Number 18-19 
approving/denying a Special Use Permit allowing for the construction of a fenced community
collection center adjacent to the current Pinetop-Lakeside Public Works building, that
will accommodate collection of household trash and provide recycling opportunities to the public
on a parcel which is zoned A-General, APN 212-38-020, a portion of Township 9 North, Range
22 East, Section 35, in the Pinetop-Lakeside area. 
Background:
The applicant is requesting a Special Use Permit allowing for the construction of a fenced, solid
waste and recycling facility where the subject parcel is located immediately west of Woodland
Park.  The subject property is on what is considered a "County Island".  Due to the proximity to
the Pinetop-Lakeside Planning area, it is important to consider the compatibility of use for land in
this area with land in Pinetop-Lakeside.  Based on the Pinetop-Lakeside Land Use Map, this land
and its surrounding area are intended for the Community Commercial designation.  The proposed
project fits within the recommendations of this Plan.  The proposed use would be permitted in the
A-General zoning district by approval of a Special Use Permit.  The collection facility will create
a slight increase in traffic.  Planning & Zoning Commission recommended approval to the Board
by a vote of 6 yeas and 0 nay.
 
Recommendation: 
Staff recommends approval of this Special Use Permit under the following stipulations: 
The Special Use Permit shall permit the collection center and accessory structures
necessary for the operation on the subject property.
1.
The permitted Special Use shall be allowed to occur only in the location shown on the
approved site plan.
2.
The Special Use Permit shall run with the land.3.
The applicant must meet all local, State and Federal requirements prior to any building
permits being issued
4.
A seven (7) foot fence shall be constructed to enclose the property.5.

NAVAJO COUNTY PUBLIC WORKS DEPARTMENT 
PLANNING & ZONING 
 
 
STAFF REPORT  
BOARD OF SUPERVISORS 
 
Hearing Date: June 11, 2019 
Case No:  19-28 
Action:   Special Use Permit 
Applicant/Owner: Town of Pinetop-Lakeside 
Project Name:  PTLS Collection Center 
PARCEL INFORMATION  
 APN:  212-38-020 
 Legal Desc: Township 9 North, Range 22 East, Section 35, in the Pinetop-Lakeside Area. 
 District: V 
 Area:  Pinetop, AZ 
 Parcel Size: 1 Acres 
STATED REASON FOR REQUEST:   
Town of Pinetop-Lakeside is requesting a Special Use Permit to construct a collection center.  The 
requested permit is to allow for the installation of a collection center adjacent to the current public 
works building, that will be fenced. 
 
GENERAL CHARACTER OF NEIGHBORHOOD:  
The subject parcel is zoned A-General and the surrounding area is primarily used for agricultural and 
residential purposes.

<!-- page 610 -->
ZONING DISTRICTS: 
ARTICLE 3 – (A-GENERAL) GENERAL ZONING DISTRICT  
Section 301 – Purpose. 
- The main purpose of this A-General Zone is to provide for all the unincorporated area of the County, except 
land within the boundary of a reservation and outside the authority of Navajo County not otherwise 
designated for some other specific zone, to be included in the “A -General Zone” by this Ordinance. No 
subdividing shall be conducted or approved in the “A -General Zone”, without prior re -zoning of the land 
so intended. Uses perm itted in the “A -General Zone”, include farm and non -farm residential uses, farms, 
recreational, institutional, commercial, and industrial uses as specifically listed in this Article. Other uses 
may be permitted as Special Uses under Article 20.  
 
ZONING ORDINANCE: 
Article: 20 – Special Uses and Planned Unit Development 
Section: 2001 – Special Uses 
 2001.29 – Recycling facilities in the Rural (RU), A-General and Commercial- 
Residential Zoning Districts. 
 
COMPREHENSIVE PLAN: 
 
1.4 Land Use  
“Given the vast area and primarily rural character of Navajo County, the character areas in the plan represent 
generalized land use, development or preservation concepts that recognize and promote existing development 
patterns.  The character areas take the place of more traditional land use categories as this Comprehensive Plan 
is intended to set a general framework for development in the County rather than establish a more precise guide 
that mirrors a zoning district map.” 
 
As it stands, the subject property is on what is considered a “County Island.” Due to its proximity to the 
Pinetop-Lakeside Planning area, it is important to consider the compatibility of use for land in this area with 
land in Pinetop-Lakeside.  Based on the Pinetop-Lakeside Land Use Map, this land and its surrounding area are 
intended for the Community Commercial designation. 
 
4.0 Land Use Element 
  
4.7 Goals and Policies 
 
Goal 2: To administer the Land Use Element of the Navajo County Comprehensive Plan so as to further the 
purposes of the Plan. 
Policy 2-B: When interpreting character area boundaries, due to the general and non-site-specific 
nature of the Land Use Map, the Planning and Zoning Commission shall consider the character of 
surrounding uses, past interpretations, and applicable goals and policies of the Comprehensive Plan. 
• Due to its location within the Pinetop-Lakeside Community Commercial designation, it is 
important to consider the proposal for the collection center in the context of other facilities in 
the area, which are currently a Public Works facility and window and door business.  
 
 
FINDINGS OF FACT: 
 
1. The item has been properly noticed and posted in compliance with Arizona Revised 
Statutes and Article 29. 
The legal notice for this item has been printed in the local newspaper, in compliance with 
Arizona Revised Statutes and Article 29 of the Zoning Ordinance.  Notices were sent to all 
parcels within a 300’ boundary from the parcel.  Public notices were also posted on the 
property.

<!-- page 611 -->
2. The proposal is in compliance with the Comprehensive Plan 
Given that this property is located within the Pinetop-Lakeside Planning Area and is part of the 
Community Commercial land use designation, the proposal for a community collection center 
fits with the goals of the Comprehensive Plan. 
3. The proposed use is compatible with the permissible uses in the zoning district in which 
the property is located. 
The proposed use is currently adjacent to a Public Works building owned by Pinetop-Lakeside 
and is looking to expand upon this operation and utilize adjacent property, making it compatible 
with the area. 
4. The proposed use is compatible with current and likely future uses of properties in the 
vicinity of the proposed use. 
As far as current uses, this property will be compatible.  Pinetop-Lakeside owns the property 
across the road from this property, within their municipal boundaries, so it would appear there 
should be no conflict of uses in the future.   
5. The proposed use will have minimal adverse effects on the public health, safety, and 
general welfare of properties in the vicinity of the proposed use. 
Given the location of the proposed facility in addition to the stipulations that have been put in 
place both by Navajo County and the Arizona Department of Environmental Quality, there 
should be no issues with health, safety, or welfare in the future with this use. 
 
ANALYSIS: 
 
As of May 30th, 2019, no concerns have been raised by notified neighbors within the area. 
 
During the May 16th, 2019 Planning and Zoning Commission Meeting, Planning and Zoning Staff 
recommended that the Navajo County Planning and Zoning Commission make the recommendation of 
approval of this Special Use Permit, subject to stipulations.  In that meeting, the Commission did 
unanimously make the recommendation to the Board of Supervisors, subject to the stipulations laid out 
within this Staff Report. 
 
 
PUBLIC WORKS AND OTHER COUNTY DEPARTMENT’S COMMENTS:  
 
COUNTY ATTORNEY: 
No objection, as long as the applicant complies with all rules and regulations of Navajo County and the 
State of Arizona. 
Initial:  B.S.C. Date:   
 
ENGINEERING & TRAFFIC: 
Engineering staff has reviewed the proposed Special Use Permit and has no Engineering objections given 
that the conditions listed below are met. 
Initial: W.R.B. Date:   
 
DRAINAGE & FLOOD CONTROL: 
The Flood Control staff has reviewed the proposed Special Use Permit and has no objections, given that 
the conditions listed below are met. 
Initial: B.P. Date:  
 
PLANNING & ZONING: 
The Planning & Zoning staff has reviewed the proposed Special Use Permit and has no objections, given 
that the conditions listed below are met. 
Initial: S.P. Date:

<!-- page 612 -->
PLANNING AND ZONING RECOMMENDATION:  
 
The Planning and Zoning Commission has unanimously recommended approval of the Special Use 
Permit to the Navajo County Board of Supervisors, subject to the following conditions: 
 
1. The Special Use Permit shall permit the collection center and accessory structures 
necessary for the operation on the subject property. 
2. The permitted Special Use shall be allowed to occur only in the location shown on the 
approved site plan. 
3. The Special Use Permit shall run with the land. 
4. The applicant must meet all local, State and Federal requirements prior to any building 
permits being issued 
5. A six (6) foot fence shall be constructed to enclose the property. 
6. If the permitted Special Use is not utilized within a twelve (12) month period from the date 
of approval by the Board of Supervisors, this permit shall become null and void.

Motion before the Board:
Contract with Atkins in the amount of $49,923 to produce plans and specifications for the next
phase of channel improvements within the Sky Hi Subdivision to further mitigate flooding in this
area
Background:
A contract proposal with Atkins in the amount of $49,923 was considered and approved at the
April 23, 2019, meeting of the Board of Supervisors. The contract referenced “Navajo County”
and was agendized under Public Works when it should have referenced “Navajo County Flood
Control District”, and been agendized accordingly. The attached agreement identifies both parties
correctly; no other change.
